# A faster-reacting scale against the clustered misses (#1153)

**Hypothesis and step:** H5 (primary goal), step 3 (fix; refocus), the clustered misses of the scaled mean reversion. **Development, not a verdict**: development days to 2025-12-31, no verdict (scoring protocol v1, #640). Any change to the candidate is Eleonora's decision. Generated by `scripts/faster_scale_1153.py page` from `docs/research/faster-scale-1153/result.json`, not edited by hand; declared in `metadata/faster_scale_1153.json` before any score.

Panel digest `4ddc3882…`. Studentized stationary bootstrap (ruling 1), mean block 10 business days, 10,000 draws, seed 1153, two-sided 90% interval; skill is `mean(d) / mean(S(benchmark))` with `d = S(benchmark) - S(candidate)`, positive favours the candidate. Coverage intervals are 90% stationary-bootstrap percentile intervals (2,000 resamples), pooled. Scored decision days: 1,855 (2018-07-19 to 2025-12-23); (a) and every arm are scored on the same days.

**What this is not.** (a) and the arms v1 to v3 read no calendar column, so their figures are provisional under the calendar-first rule (`docs/research/provisional-calendar-register.md`) and close no question in either direction; v4 reads the target day's calendar type on direct pairs. The base is #941's best variant, chosen after seeing 2018-2025. #1145 (PR #1151) has not merged and no winning arm of it exists, so this runs on (a) alone. `gbm_funding` is not compared here: its walks are a long run for Eleonora's Mac and were not supplied.

## Which arm v4 builds on

Chosen by the rule declared before any score: among v1 to v3, the arms whose pooled mean grid CRPS is at most 1% above (a)'s (1.698) are eligible, and the eligible arm with the shortest mean run of the 90% band's misses (closed rule, pooled over leads) wins; none eligible, the lowest grid CRPS wins.

| arm | pooled mean grid CRPS | eligible | mean miss run |
|---|---|---|---|
| v1 blend of 5- and 20-day MAD | 1.726 | no | 1.960 |
| v1 MAD 10 days | 1.718 | no | 2.000 |
| v1 MAD 5 days | 1.821 | no | 1.959 |
| v2 EWMA half-life 10 | 1.776 | no | 2.306 |
| v2 EWMA half-life 3 | 1.818 | no | 2.097 |
| v2 EWMA half-life 5 | 1.792 | no | 2.176 |
| v2 EWMA half-life 5, asymmetric | 1.787 | no | 2.305 |
| v3 miss feedback | 1.696 | yes | 1.973 |

Winner: **v3 miss feedback**; v4 is `v4_v3_miss_cal`.

## The clustering of the 90% band's misses (the headline of this fix)

Per lead, pooled over the scored days: the miss rate (nominal 0.10), then Christoffersen's independence p and the conditional coverage p. A p of 0.000 is below 0.0005. Reporting only: no p-value is a gate.

### Miss runs

A run is a block of consecutive misses in a lead's sequence of consecutive decision days. Pooled over the five leads: the number of runs, the mean run length (misses per run; about 1.11 for independent misses at a 10% rate) and the longest run, for the 90% band and for q95 (above its quantile).

## Against the kill rule

The kill rule (the issue's, read in the declaration): stop if no arm moves the independence p-value off 0.000 at any lead and no arm shortens the miss runs. 'Off 0.000' is a pooled independence p of the 90% band of at least 0.0005, at any lead, under either hit rule; 'shortens' is a mean run (closed rule, pooled over leads) below (a)'s (2.205). The arms are provisional (the calendar-blind ones), so a kill would close no question.

| arm | leads off 0.000, `above` | leads off 0.000, `at_or_above` | mean run, `above` (longest) | mean run, `at_or_above` (longest) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) base (#941 mad20) | none | none | 2.205 (13) | 2.205 (13) |
| v1 MAD 5 days | none | none | 1.959 (7) | 1.957 (7) |
| v1 MAD 10 days | none | none | 2.000 (10) | 2.000 (10) |
| v1 blend of 5- and 20-day MAD | none | none | 1.960 (9) | 1.958 (9) |
| v2 EWMA half-life 3 | none | none | 2.097 (10) | 2.097 (10) |
| v2 EWMA half-life 5 | none | none | 2.176 (10) | 2.176 (10) |
| v2 EWMA half-life 10 | none | none | 2.306 (11) | 2.310 (11) |
| v2 EWMA half-life 5, asymmetric | none | none | 2.305 (11) | 2.309 (11) |
| v3 miss feedback | none | none | 1.973 (7) | 1.979 (7) |
| v4 v3 miss feedback + calendar type | none | none | 1.987 (7) | 1.985 (7) |

Arms with a lead off 0.000: none. Arms with a shorter mean run than (a): v1 MAD 5 days, v1 MAD 10 days, v1 blend of 5- and 20-day MAD, v2 EWMA half-life 3, v2 EWMA half-life 5, v3 miss feedback, v4 v3 miss feedback + calendar type. **The kill rule is not met.**

## Reading under the declared rule

A gain (loss) is a grid-CRPS skill over (a) whose 90% interval is above (below) zero; otherwise undecided.

- **v1 MAD 5 days**: all decision days: loss; 2018-19: loss; 2020: undecided; 2021-23: undecided; 2024: undecided; 2025-26: loss; quarter_end: undecided; month_end: loss; tax_date: undecided; ordinary: loss.
- **v1 MAD 10 days**: all decision days: undecided; 2018-19: undecided; 2020: gain; 2021-23: undecided; 2024: undecided; 2025-26: loss; quarter_end: undecided; month_end: loss; tax_date: undecided; ordinary: undecided.

## Band coverage

Share of (decision day, lead) cells whose outcome is inside the band, with its 90% interval (pooled). Closed: `q_lo <= y <= q_hi` (the hit rule `y > q`); open: `q_lo < y < q_hi` (the rule `y >= q`). Nominal 0.50, 0.80, 0.90, 0.98. The hit rule is Eleonora's open question (#993) and both are reported.

## Exceedances and band misses, by lead, under both hit rules

Each cell is the rate (nominal in the heading) with the p-values of Kupiec's unconditional coverage test, Christoffersen's independence test and his conditional coverage test (`repo_model.coverage_tests`). Upper tails: the outcome above q95 / q99; lower tails: below q05 / q01; a band miss: outside the band. Reporting only: no p-value is a gate. By regime and pressure-day type, the five leads together with Kupiec's p; the independence tests per regime and lead are in `result.json`.

## Against gbm_funding

Not scored: the five walks of `gbm_funding` (#634) are a long run for Eleonora's Mac and were not supplied (`--walks`). The comparison runs unchanged on them; nothing else on this page depends on it.
